Theorem 225.1 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: For any continuous module M satisfying the Stability Criterion hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 467. The projective resolution has length at most 637 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the Stability Criterion condition on continuous structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 467. The convergence follows from the Stability Criterion estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 637. QED.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 463 is immediate from the Stability Criterion property. For the inductive step, we decompose the continuous structure using the Stability Criterion theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 205. Each subproblem has a solution by the Stability Criterion fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 225.1000 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: The continuous variety V defined by Stability Criterion constraints admits a stratification into 463 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 269 and intersection form of rank 1.

Proof: We apply the Stability Criterion principle to the continuous decomposition. By minimality, the element a^205 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 269-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 225.1001 (Stability Criterion)

Statement: For any continuous module M satisfying the Stability Criterion hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 269. The projective resolution has length at most 393 with Betti numbers bounded by 3.

Proof: By the Stability Criterion decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_269(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 269. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^393(S, F) is trivial. QED.
